<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">For the Paraguayan footballer, see <a href="/wiki/%C3%93scar_Romero_(footballer)" title="Óscar Romero (footballer)">Óscar Romero (footballer)</a>. For the American soccer player, see <a href="/wiki/Oscar_Romero_(soccer)" title="Oscar Romero (soccer)">Oscar Romero (soccer)</a>.</div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">In this <a href="/wiki/Spanish_name" class="mw-redirect" title="Spanish name">Spanish name</a>, the first or paternal&#32;<a href="/wiki/Surname" title="Surname">surname</a> is <i> <a href="/wiki/Romero" title="Romero">Romero</a></i>&#32;and the second or maternal family name is <i> Galdámez</i>.</div>

  <span style="font-size:100%">Ordination history</span></div></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"><table class="infobox mw-collapsible mw-collapsed" style="background-color: transparent; background-color:#eee"><span style="font-size:100%;">History</span></th></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background:#f2f2f2;"><div style="background:#f2f2f2; border-bottom: 3px solid #333; border-collapse:collapse; border-spacing:0px; border:none; width:100%; margin:0px; font-size:100%; clear:none; float:none"></table></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below" style="font-size:smaller;"><b>Source(s):</b><sup id="cite_ref-catholic-heirarchy_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholic-heirarchy-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup></td></tr><tr style="display:none"><td colspan="2">
  </td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Óscar Arnulfo Romero y Galdámez</b> (15 August 1917 – 24 March 1980) was a <a href="/wiki/Prelate" title="Prelate">prelate</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_El_Salvador" title="Catholic Church in El Salvador">Catholic Church in El Salvador</a>. He served as Auxiliary Bishop of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_San_Salvador" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of San Salvador">Archdiocese of San Salvador</a>, the Titular Bishop of Tambeae, as <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Santiago_de_Mar%C3%ADa" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Santiago de María">Bishop of Santiago de María</a>, and finally as the fourth Archbishop of San Salvador. As archbishop, Romero spoke out against social injustice and violence amid the escalating conflict between the <a href="/wiki/Military_government" title="Military government">military government</a> and left-wing insurgents that led to the <a href="/wiki/Salvadoran_Civil_War" title="Salvadoran Civil War">Salvadoran Civil War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> In 1980, Romero was shot by an assassin while celebrating <a href="/wiki/Mass_(liturgy)" title="Mass (liturgy)">Mass</a>. Though no one was ever convicted for the crime, investigations by the UN-created <a href="/wiki/Truth_Commission_for_El_Salvador" title="Truth Commission for El Salvador">Truth Commission for El Salvador</a> concluded that Major <a href="/wiki/Roberto_D%27Aubuisson" title="Roberto D&#39;Aubuisson">Roberto D'Aubuisson</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Death_squads_in_El_Salvador" title="Death squads in El Salvador">death squad</a> leader and later founder of the right-wing <a href="/wiki/Nationalist_Republican_Alliance" title="Nationalist Republican Alliance">Nationalist Republican Alliance</a> (ARENA) political party, had ordered the killing.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1997, <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> bestowed upon Romero the title of <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God#Catholic_Church" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a>, and a cause for his <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatification</a> was opened by the church. The cause stalled, but was reopened by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> in 2012. Romero was declared a <a href="/wiki/Martyr" title="Martyr">martyr</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> on 3 February 2015, paving the way for his beatification on 23 May 2015. During Romero's beatification, Pope Francis declared that his "ministry was distinguished by his particular attention to the most poor and marginalized."<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Pope Francis <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> Romero on 14 October 2018.
</p><p>Seen as a social conservative at the time of his appointment as archbishop in 1977, Romero was deeply affected by the murder of his friend and fellow priest <a href="/wiki/Rutilio_Grande" title="Rutilio Grande">Rutilio Grande</a> and thereafter became an outspoken critic of the <a href="/wiki/Military_dictatorship_in_El_Salvador" title="Military dictatorship in El Salvador">military government of El Salvador</a>. Hailed by supporters of <a href="/wiki/Liberation_theology" title="Liberation theology">liberation theology</a>, Romero, according to his biographer, "was not interested in liberation theology" but faithfully adhered to Catholic teachings on liberation and a <a href="/wiki/Preferential_option_for_the_poor" class="mw-redirect" title="Preferential option for the poor">preferential option for the poor</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> desiring a social revolution based on interior reform. Up to the end of his life, his spiritual life drew much from the spirituality of <a href="/wiki/Opus_Dei" title="Opus Dei">Opus Dei</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2010, the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly" title="United Nations General Assembly">United Nations General Assembly</a> proclaimed 24 March as the "International Day for the Right to the Truth Concerning Gross Human Rights Violations and for the Dignity of Victims" in recognition of Romero's role in defense of human rights. Romero actively denounced violations of the human rights of the most vulnerable people and defended the principles of protecting lives, promoting human dignity and opposing all forms of violence. Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Luis_Escobar_Alas" title="José Luis Escobar Alas">José Luis Escobar Alas</a>, one of Romero's successors as Metropolitan Archbishop of the Roman Catholic Archdiocese of San Salvador, El Salvador, asked Pope Francis to proclaim Romero a <a href="/wiki/Doctor_of_the_Church" title="Doctor of the Church">Doctor of the Church</a>, which is an acknowledgement from the church that his religious teachings were orthodox and had a significant impact on its philosophy and theology.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Latin American church groups often proclaim Romero an unofficial <a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">patron saint</a> of <a href="/wiki/The_Americas" class="mw-redirect" title="The Americas">the Americas</a> and El Salvador; Catholics in El Salvador often refer to him as <i>San Romero</i>, as well as <i>Monseñor Romero</i>. Outside of Catholicism, Romero is honored by other <a href="/wiki/Christendom" title="Christendom">Christian denominations</a>, including the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> and <a href="/wiki/Anglican_Communion" title="Anglican Communion">Anglican Communion</a>, through the Calendar in <a href="/wiki/Common_Worship" title="Common Worship">Common Worship</a>, as well as in at least one <a href="/wiki/Lutheran_liturgical_calendar" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutheran liturgical calendar">Lutheran liturgical calendar</a>. Romero is also one of the ten 20th-century martyrs depicted in statues above the Great West Door of <a href="/wiki/Westminster_Abbey" title="Westminster Abbey">Westminster Abbey</a> in London.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:MOAR_3.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a0/MOAR_3.jpg/170px-MOAR_3.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="239" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a0/MOAR_3.jpg/255px-MOAR_3.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/a0/MOAR_3.jpg 2x" data-file-width="285" data-file-height="400" /></a><figcaption>Romero in 1941</figcaption></figure>
<p>Óscar Romero was born on 15 August 1917<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> to Santos Romero and Guadalupe de Jesús Galdámez in <a href="/wiki/Ciudad_Barrios" title="Ciudad Barrios">Ciudad Barrios</a> in the <a href="/wiki/San_Miguel_Department_(El_Salvador)" title="San Miguel Department (El Salvador)">San Miguel department</a> of <a href="/wiki/El_Salvador" title="El Salvador">El Salvador</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> On 11 May 1919, at the age of one, Romero was baptized into the Catholic Church by the priest Cecilio Morales.<sup id="cite_ref-Brockman1989_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brockman1989-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Romero entered the local public school, which offered only grades one through three. When finished with public school, Romero was privately tutored by a teacher, Anita Iglesias,<sup id="cite_ref-Brockman1982_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brockman1982-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> until the age of thirteen.<sup id="cite_ref-Brockman2005_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brockman2005-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> During this time Romero's father trained him in carpentry.<sup id="cite_ref-Royal2000_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Royal2000-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Romero showed exceptional proficiency as an apprentice. His father wanted to offer his son the skill of a trade, because in El Salvador studies seldom led to employment,<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> however, Romero broached the idea of studying for the priesthood, which did not surprise those who knew him.<sup id="cite_ref-liberators_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-liberators-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Priesthood">Priesthood</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Priesthood">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Romero,_Vatican_City,_1942,_color.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Romero%2C_Vatican_City%2C_1942%2C_color.jpg/170px-Romero%2C_Vatican_City%2C_1942%2C_color.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="255" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Romero%2C_Vatican_City%2C_1942%2C_color.jpg/255px-Romero%2C_Vatican_City%2C_1942%2C_color.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Romero%2C_Vatican_City%2C_1942%2C_color.jpg/340px-Romero%2C_Vatican_City%2C_1942%2C_color.jpg 2x" data-file-width="700" data-file-height="1050" /></a><figcaption>Romero in 1942 at <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">the Vatican</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Romero entered the <a href="/wiki/Minor_seminary" title="Minor seminary">minor seminary</a> in San Miguel at the age of thirteen. He left the seminary for three months to return home when his mother became ill after the birth of her eighth child; during this time he worked with two of his brothers in a gold mine near Ciudad Barrios.<sup id="cite_ref-liberators_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-liberators-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> After graduation, he enrolled in the national <a href="/wiki/Seminary" title="Seminary">seminary</a> in San Salvador. He completed his studies at the <a href="/wiki/Gregorian_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Gregorian University">Gregorian University</a> in <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>, where he received a <a href="/wiki/Licentiate_in_Theology" class="mw-redirect" title="Licentiate in Theology">Licentiate in Theology</a> <i>cum laude</i> in 1941, but had to wait a year to be ordained because he was younger than the required age.<sup id="cite_ref-wright_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wright-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> He was <a href="/wiki/Holy_Orders" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy Orders">ordained</a> in Rome on 4 April 1942.<sup id="cite_ref-catholic-heirarchy_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholic-heirarchy-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-un_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-un-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> His family could not attend his ordination because of travel restrictions due to <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-kell-bio_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kell-bio-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Romero remained in Italy to obtain a doctoral degree in theology, specializing in <a href="/wiki/Ascetical_theology" title="Ascetical theology">ascetical theology</a> and Christian perfection according to <a href="/wiki/Luis_de_la_Puente" title="Luis de la Puente">Luis de la Puente</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-wright_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wright-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Before finishing, in 1943 at the age of 26, he was summoned back home from Italy by his bishop. He traveled home with a good friend, Father Valladares, who was also doing doctoral work in Rome. On the route home, they made stops in Spain and Cuba, where they were detained by the Cuban police, likely for having come from <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy#Fascist_regime_(1922–1943)" title="Kingdom of Italy">Fascist Italy</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> and were placed in a series of internment camps. After several months in prison, Valladares became sick and <a href="/wiki/Congregation_of_the_Most_Holy_Redeemer" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation of the Most Holy Redeemer">Redemptorist</a> priests helped to have the two transferred to a hospital. From the hospital they were released from Cuban custody and sailed on to Mexico, then traveled overland to El Salvador.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Romero was first assigned to serve as a <a href="/wiki/Parish_priest" class="mw-redirect" title="Parish priest">parish priest</a> in <a href="/wiki/Anamor%C3%B3s" title="Anamorós">Anamorós</a>, but then moved to <a href="/wiki/San_Miguel,_El_Salvador" title="San Miguel, El Salvador">San Miguel</a> where he worked for over 20 years.<sup id="cite_ref-un_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-un-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> He promoted various apostolic groups, started an <a href="/wiki/Alcoholics_Anonymous" title="Alcoholics Anonymous">Alcoholics Anonymous</a> group, helped in the construction of San Miguel's cathedral, and supported devotion to <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_Peace" title="Our Lady of Peace">Our Lady of Peace</a>. He was later appointed rector of the inter-diocesan seminary in <a href="/wiki/San_Salvador" title="San Salvador">San Salvador</a>. Emotionally and physically exhausted by his work in San Miguel, Romero took a retreat in January 1966 where he visited a priest for confession and a psychiatrist. He was diagnosed by the psychiatrist as having <a href="/wiki/Obsessive-compulsive_personality_disorder" class="mw-redirect" title="Obsessive-compulsive personality disorder">obsessive-compulsive personality disorder</a> and by priests with <a href="/wiki/Scrupulosity" title="Scrupulosity">scrupulosity</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1966, he was chosen to be Secretary of the Bishops Conference for El Salvador. He also became the director of the archdiocesan newspaper <i>Orientación</i>, which became fairly conservative while he was editor, defending the traditional Magisterium of the Catholic Church.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Bishop_and_Archbishop">Bishop and Archbishop</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Bishop and Archbishop">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>On 25 April 1970, Romero was appointed an <a href="/wiki/Auxiliary_bishop" title="Auxiliary bishop">auxiliary bishop</a> for the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_San_Salvador" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of San Salvador">Archdiocese of San Salvador</a> and as the titular bishop of Tambeae.<sup id="cite_ref-catholic-heirarchy_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholic-heirarchy-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> He was consecrated on 21 June by <a href="/wiki/Girolamo_Prigione" title="Girolamo Prigione">Girolamo Prigione</a>, titular Archbishop of Lauriacum.<sup id="cite_ref-catholic-heirarchy_3-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholic-heirarchy-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> On 15 October 1974, he was appointed Bishop of the <a href="/wiki/Diocese_of_Santiago_de_Mar%C3%ADa" class="mw-redirect" title="Diocese of Santiago de María">Diocese of Santiago de María</a>, a poor, rural region.<sup id="cite_ref-catholic-heirarchy_3-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholic-heirarchy-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-un_20-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-un-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 3 February 1977, Romero was appointed Archbishop of San Salvador, assuming the position on 22 February.<sup id="cite_ref-catholic-heirarchy_3-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholic-heirarchy-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> While this appointment was welcomed by the government, many priests were disappointed, especially those openly supportive of <a href="/wiki/Marxism" title="Marxism">Marxist</a> ideology. The progressive priests feared that his conservative reputation would negatively affect <a href="/wiki/Liberation_theology" title="Liberation theology">liberation theology</a>'s commitment to the poor.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mural_Oscar_Romero_UES.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Mural_Oscar_Romero_UES.jpg/170px-Mural_Oscar_Romero_UES.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Mural_Oscar_Romero_UES.jpg/255px-Mural_Oscar_Romero_UES.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Mural_Oscar_Romero_UES.jpg/340px-Mural_Oscar_Romero_UES.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2112" data-file-height="2816" /></a><figcaption>A mural of Óscar Romero</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 12 March 1977, <a href="/wiki/Rutilio_Grande" title="Rutilio Grande">Rutilio Grande</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Jesus" class="mw-redirect" title="Society of Jesus">Jesuit</a> priest and personal friend of Romero who had been creating self-reliance groups among the poor, was assassinated. His death had a profound impact on Romero, who later stated: "When I looked at Rutilio lying there dead I thought, 'If they have killed him for doing what he did, then I too have to walk the same path.'"<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Romero urged the government to investigate, but they ignored his request. Furthermore, the censored press remained silent.<sup id="cite_ref-infed.org_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-infed.org-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Tension was noted by the closure of schools and the lack of Catholic priests invited to participate in government. In response to Grande's murder, Romero revealed an activism that had not been evident earlier, speaking out against poverty, social injustice, assassinations and torture.<sup id="cite_ref-Eaton_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eaton-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 15 October 1979, the <a href="/wiki/Revolutionary_Government_Junta_of_El_Salvador" title="Revolutionary Government Junta of El Salvador">Revolutionary Government Junta</a> (JRG) came to power amidst a wave of human rights abuses by paramilitary right-wing groups and the government, in an escalation of violence that would become the <a href="/wiki/Salvadoran_Civil_War" title="Salvadoran Civil War">Salvadoran Civil War</a>. Romero criticized the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a> for giving military aid to the new government and wrote an open letter to President <a href="/wiki/Jimmy_Carter" title="Jimmy Carter">Jimmy Carter</a> in February 1980, warning that increased US military aid would "undoubtedly sharpen the injustice and the political repression inflicted on the organized people, whose struggle has often been for their most basic human rights." This letter was then sent, via telegram, from the U.S. embassy in El Salvador to Washington D.C.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Carter did not directly respond to the letter; instead, <a href="/wiki/Cyrus_Vance" title="Cyrus Vance">Cyrus Vance</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_state_(U.S._state_government)" title="Secretary of state (U.S. state government)">Secretary of State</a>, wrote a telegram back to the U.S. embassy. The telegram carried a very contradictory message, both stating that the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a> will not interfere but will respond to the Revolutionary Government Junta's requests. It is unknown if Archbishop Romero received the telegram.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 11 May 1979, Romero met with <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> and unsuccessfully attempted to obtain a Vatican condemnation of the Salvadoran military regime for committing human rights violations and its support of <a href="/wiki/Death_squads_in_El_Salvador" title="Death squads in El Salvador">death squads</a>, and expressed his frustration in working with clergy who cooperated with the government. He was encouraged by Pope John Paul II to maintain episcopal unity as a top priority.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-infed.org_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-infed.org-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a result of his humanitarian efforts, Romero began to be noticed internationally. In February 1980, he was given an honorary doctorate by the <a href="/wiki/Universit%C3%A9_catholique_de_Louvain" title="Université catholique de Louvain">Catholic University of Louvain</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Statements_on_persecution_of_the_church">Statements on persecution of the church</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Statements on persecution of the church">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Oscar_Romero_by_puigreixach.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Oscar_Romero_by_puigreixach.jpg/170px-Oscar_Romero_by_puigreixach.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="230" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Oscar_Romero_by_puigreixach.jpg/255px-Oscar_Romero_by_puigreixach.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ca/Oscar_Romero_by_puigreixach.jpg/340px-Oscar_Romero_by_puigreixach.jpg 2x" data-file-width="808" data-file-height="1091" /></a><figcaption>Óscar Romero (pastel) by J. Puig Reixach (2013)</figcaption></figure>
<p>Romero denounced the persecution of members of the Catholic Church who had worked on behalf of the poor:<sup id="cite_ref-Louvaine_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Louvaine-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>In less than three years, more than fifty priests have been attacked, threatened, calumniated. Six are already martyrs—they were murdered. Some have been tortured and others expelled [from the country]. Nuns have also been persecuted. The archdiocesan radio station and educational institutions that are Catholic or of a Christian inspiration have been attacked, threatened, intimidated, even bombed. Several parish communities have been raided. If all this has happened to persons who are the most evident representatives of the Church, you can guess what has happened to ordinary Christians, to the campesinos, catechists, lay ministers, and to the ecclesial base communities. There have been threats, arrests, tortures, murders, numbering in the hundreds and thousands....
But it is important to note why [the Church] has been persecuted. Not any and every priest has been persecuted, not any and every institution has been attacked. That part of the church has been attacked and persecuted that put itself on the side of the people and went to the people's defense. Here again we find the same key to understanding the persecution of the church: the poor.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite>Óscar Romero, Speech at the <a href="/wiki/Universit%C3%A9_catholique_de_Louvain" title="Université catholique de Louvain">Université catholique de Louvain</a>, <a href="/wiki/Belgium" title="Belgium">Belgium</a>, 2 February 1980.</cite></div></blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Popular_radio_sermons">Popular radio sermons</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Popular radio sermons">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:O.Romero_1979_autographed_photo.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/O.Romero_1979_autographed_photo.jpg/220px-O.Romero_1979_autographed_photo.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="275" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/O.Romero_1979_autographed_photo.jpg/330px-O.Romero_1979_autographed_photo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/16/O.Romero_1979_autographed_photo.jpg/440px-O.Romero_1979_autographed_photo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="750" /></a><figcaption>Romero in 1979.</figcaption></figure>
<p>By the time of his death, Romero had gained an enormous following among Salvadorans. He did this largely through broadcasting his weekly sermons across El Salvador<sup id="cite_ref-Peadar_Kirby_1980_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Peadar_Kirby_1980-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> on the church's station, YSAX, "except when it was bombed off the air."<sup id="cite_ref-diary_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-diary-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> In these sermons, he listed disappearances, tortures, murders, and much more each Sunday.<sup id="cite_ref-Peadar_Kirby_1980_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Peadar_Kirby_1980-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> This was followed by an hour-long speech on radio the following day. On the importance of these broadcasts, one writer noted that "the archbishop's Sunday sermon was the main source in El Salvador about what was happening. It was estimated to have the largest listenership of any programme in the country."<sup id="cite_ref-Peadar_Kirby_1980_38-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Peadar_Kirby_1980-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> According to listener surveys, 73% of the rural population and 47% of the urban listened regularly.<sup id="cite_ref-diary_39-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-diary-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Similarly, his diocesan weekly paper <i>Orientación</i> carried lists of cases of torture and repression every week.<sup id="cite_ref-Peadar_Kirby_1980_38-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Peadar_Kirby_1980-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Theology">Theology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Theology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>According to Jesús Delgado, his biographer and <a href="/wiki/Postulator" title="Postulator">postulator</a> of the cause for his canonization, Romero agreed with the Catholic vision of liberation theology and not with the materialist vision: "A journalist once asked him: 'Do you agree with Liberation Theology' And Romero answered: "Yes, of course. However, there are two theologies of liberation. One is that which sees liberation only as material liberation. The other is that of Paul VI. I am with Paul VI."<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Delgado said that Romero did not read the books on liberation theology which he received, and he gave the lowest priority to liberation theology among the topics that he studied.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Romero preached that "the most profound social revolution is the serious, supernatural, interior reform of a Christian."<sup id="cite_ref-A._Romero_p._1_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-A._Romero_p._1-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> He also emphasized: "The liberation of Christ and of His Church is not reduced to the dimension of a purely temporal project. It does not reduce its objectives to an anthropocentric perspective: to a material well-being or only to initiatives of a political or social, economic or cultural order. Much less can it be a liberation that supports or is supported by violence."<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> Romero expressed several times his disapproval of divisiveness in the church. In a sermon preached on 11 November 1979 he said: "the other day, one of the persons who proclaims liberation in a political sense was asked: 'For you, what is the meaning of the Church'?" He said that the activist "answered with these scandalous words: 'There are two churches, the church of the rich and the church of the poor. We believe in the church of the poor but not in the church of the rich.'" Romero declared, "Clearly these words are a form of demagogy and I will never admit a division of the Church." He added, "There is only one Church, the Church that Christ preached, the Church to which we should give our whole hearts. There is only one Church, a Church that adores the living God and knows how to give relative value to the goods of this earth."<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Spiritual_life">Spiritual life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Spiritual life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_with_Pope_Paul_VI_(2)_cropped.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_with_Pope_Paul_VI_%282%29_cropped.png/220px-%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_with_Pope_Paul_VI_%282%29_cropped.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="226" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/fd/%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_with_Pope_Paul_VI_%282%29_cropped.png 1.5x" data-file-width="313" data-file-height="321" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a> and Romero, 1978</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_with_Pope_John_Paul_II_(2).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_with_Pope_John_Paul_II_%282%29.jpg/220px-%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_with_Pope_John_Paul_II_%282%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="178" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_with_Pope_John_Paul_II_%282%29.jpg/330px-%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_with_Pope_John_Paul_II_%282%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_with_Pope_John_Paul_II_%282%29.jpg/440px-%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_with_Pope_John_Paul_II_%282%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="494" data-file-height="400" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/John_Paul_II" class="mw-redirect" title="John Paul II">John Paul II</a> and Romero, 1979</figcaption></figure>
<p>Romero noted in his diary on 4 February 1943: "In recent days the Lord has inspired in me a great desire for holiness. I have been thinking of how far a soul can ascend if it lets itself be possessed entirely by God." Commenting on this passage, James R. Brockman, Romero's biographer and author of <i>Romero: A Life</i>, said that "All the evidence available indicates that he continued on his quest for holiness until the end of his life. But he also matured in that quest."<sup id="cite_ref-spirjourn_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-spirjourn-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to Brockman, Romero's spiritual journey had some of these characteristics:
</p>
<ul><li>love for the Church of Rome, shown by his episcopal motto, "to be of one mind with the Church," a phrase he took from <a href="/wiki/Ignatius_of_Loyola" title="Ignatius of Loyola">St. Ignatius</a>' <i><a href="/wiki/Spiritual_Exercises" title="Spiritual Exercises">Spiritual Exercises</a></i>;</li>
<li>a tendency to make a very deep <a href="/wiki/Examination_of_conscience" title="Examination of conscience">examination of conscience</a>;</li>
<li>an emphasis on sincere <a href="/wiki/Piety" title="Piety">piety</a>;</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Mortification_in_Roman_Catholic_teaching" class="mw-redirect" title="Mortification in Roman Catholic teaching">mortification</a> and <a href="/wiki/Penance" title="Penance">penance</a> through his duties;</li>
<li>providing protection for his <a href="/wiki/Chastity" title="Chastity">chastity</a>;</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Spiritual_direction" title="Spiritual direction">spiritual direction</a>;</li>
<li>"being one with the Church incarnated in this people which stands in need of liberation";</li>
<li>eagerness for contemplative prayer and finding God in others;</li>
<li>fidelity to the <a href="/wiki/Will_of_God" title="Will of God">will of God</a>;</li>
<li>self-offering to <a href="/wiki/Jesus_Christ" class="mw-redirect" title="Jesus Christ">Jesus Christ</a>.</li></ul>
<p>Romero was a strong advocate of the spiritual charism of <a href="/wiki/Opus_Dei" title="Opus Dei">Opus Dei</a>. He received weekly spiritual direction from a priest of the Opus Dei movement.<sup id="cite_ref-cruxnow.com_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cruxnow.com-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> In 1975 he wrote in support of the cause of canonization of Opus Dei's founder, "Personally, I owe deep gratitude to the priests involved with the Work, to whom I have entrusted with much satisfaction the spiritual direction of my own life and that of other priests."<sup id="cite_ref-Opus_Dei_archives_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Opus_Dei_archives-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Assassination">Assassination</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Assassination">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Assassination_of_Oscar_Romero.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/c/c6/Assassination_of_Oscar_Romero.jpg/220px-Assassination_of_Oscar_Romero.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="299" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/c/c6/Assassination_of_Oscar_Romero.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="271" data-file-height="368" /></a><figcaption>Photo that appeared in <i><a href="/wiki/El_Pa%C3%ADs" title="El País">El País</a></i> on 7 November 2009 with the information that the state of <a href="/wiki/El_Salvador" title="El Salvador">El Salvador</a> recognized its responsibility in the crime.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>On 23 March 1980, Archbishop Romero delivered a sermon in which he called on Salvadoran soldiers, as <a href="/wiki/Christians" title="Christians">Christians</a>, to obey God's higher order and to stop carrying out the government's repression and violations of basic <a href="/wiki/Human_rights" title="Human rights">human rights</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_News_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_News-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-kell-bio_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kell-bio-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Romero spent 24 March in a recollection organized by Opus Dei,<sup id="cite_ref-auto_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> a monthly gathering of priest friends led by <a href="/wiki/Fernando_S%C3%A1enz_Lacalle" title="Fernando Sáenz Lacalle">Fernando Sáenz Lacalle</a>. On that day they reflected on the priesthood.<sup id="cite_ref-romereports.com_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-romereports.com-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> That evening, Romero celebrated <a href="/wiki/Mass_(liturgy)" title="Mass (liturgy)">Mass</a><sup id="cite_ref-Gómez2003_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gómez2003-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Settimba2009_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Settimba2009-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> at a small chapel at Hospital de la Divina Providencia (Divine Providence Hospital),<sup id="cite_ref-nytimesmurder_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nytimesmurder-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> a church-run hospital specializing in oncology and care for the terminally ill.<sup id="cite_ref-wapomurder_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wapomurder-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Romero finished his sermon, stepped away from the <a href="/wiki/Lectern" title="Lectern">lectern</a>, and took a few steps to stand at the center of the altar.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_News_51-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_News-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As Romero finished speaking, a red car came to a stop on the street in front of the chapel. A gunman emerged from the vehicle, stepped to the door of the chapel, and fired one, possibly two, shots. Romero was struck in the heart, and the vehicle sped off.<sup id="cite_ref-nytimesmurder_55-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nytimesmurder-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> He died at the Chapel of Hospital de la Divina Providencia in San Salvador.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Funeral">Funeral</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Funeral">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Romero was buried in the <a href="/wiki/San_Salvador_Cathedral" title="San Salvador Cathedral">Metropolitan Cathedral of San Salvador</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Requiem_Mass" class="mw-redirect" title="Requiem Mass">Funeral Mass</a> on 30 March 1980 in San Salvador was attended by more than 250,000 mourners from all over the world. Viewing this attendance as a protest, <a href="/wiki/Jesuit" class="mw-redirect" title="Jesuit">Jesuit</a> priest <a href="/wiki/John_Dear" title="John Dear">John Dear</a> has said, "Romero's funeral was the largest demonstration in Salvadoran history, some say in the history of <a href="/wiki/Latin_America" title="Latin America">Latin America</a>."<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>At the funeral, <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholic_Church)" title="Cardinal (Catholic Church)">Cardinal</a> <a href="/wiki/Ernesto_Corripio_y_Ahumada" title="Ernesto Corripio y Ahumada">Ernesto Corripio y Ahumada</a>, speaking as the personal delegate of Pope John Paul II, eulogized Romero as a "beloved, peacemaking man of God," and stated that "his blood will give fruit to brotherhood, love and peace."<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Massacre_at_Romero.27s_funeral"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Massacre_at_Romero's_funeral">Massacre at Romero's funeral</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Massacre at Romero&#039;s funeral">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>During the ceremony, smoke bombs exploded on the streets near the cathedral and subsequently there were rifle shots that came from surrounding buildings, including the <a href="/wiki/National_Palace_(El_Salvador)" title="National Palace (El Salvador)">National Palace</a>. Many people were killed by gunfire and in the stampede of people running away from the explosions and gunfire. Official sources reported 31 overall casualties, while journalists claimed that between 30 and 50 died.<sup id="cite_ref-Morozzo_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Morozzo-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Some witnesses claimed it was government security forces who threw bombs into the crowd, and army sharpshooters, dressed as civilians, who fired into the chaos from the balcony or roof of the National Palace, however, there are contradictory accounts about the course of the events and one historian, Roberto Morozzo della Rocca, stated that "probably, one will never know the truth about the interrupted funeral."<sup id="cite_ref-Morozzo_59-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Morozzo-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As the gunfire continued, Romero's body was buried in a crypt beneath the sanctuary. Even after the burial, people continued to line up to pay homage to the assassinated prelate.<sup id="cite_ref-kell-bio_21-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kell-bio-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-kell-chron_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kell-chron-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-requiem_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-requiem-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-40killed_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40killed-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="International_reaction">International reaction</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: International reaction">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Ireland">Ireland</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Ireland">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>All sections of Irish political and religious life condemned his assassination, with the Minister for Foreign Affairs <a href="/wiki/Brian_Lenihan_Snr" title="Brian Lenihan Snr">Brian Lenihan</a> "expressing shock and revulsion at the murder of Dr Romero,"<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> while the leader of the <a href="/wiki/Tr%C3%B3caire" title="Trócaire">Trócaire</a> charity, Bishop <a href="/wiki/Eamon_Casey" class="mw-redirect" title="Eamon Casey">Eamon Casey</a>, revealed that he had received a letter from Romero that very day.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> The previous October, parliamentarians had given their support to the nomination of Romero for the Nobel Peace Prize.<sup id="cite_ref-ReferenceA_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ReferenceA-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> In March each year since the 1980s, the Irish–El Salvador Support Committee holds a mass in honour of Romero.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="United_Kingdom">United Kingdom</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: United Kingdom">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In October 1978, 119 British parliamentarians had nominated Romero for the Nobel Prize for Peace. In this they were supported by 26 members of the United States Congress.<sup id="cite_ref-Peadar_Kirby_1980_38-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Peadar_Kirby_1980-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> When news of the assassination was reported in March 1980, the new <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Canterbury" title="Archbishop of Canterbury">Archbishop of Canterbury</a>, <a href="/wiki/Robert_Runcie" title="Robert Runcie">Robert Runcie</a>, was about to be enthroned in <a href="/wiki/Canterbury_Cathedral" title="Canterbury Cathedral">Canterbury Cathedral</a>. On hearing of Romero's death, one writer observed that Runcie "departed from the ancient traditions to decry the murder of Archbishop Óscar Romero in El Salvador."<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="United_States">United States</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: United States">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Public_reaction">Public reaction</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Public reaction">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h5>
<p>The United States public's reaction to Archbishop Romero's death was symbolized through the "martyrdom of Romero" as an inspiration to end US military aid to El Salvador. In December 1980 the <a href="/wiki/International_Longshore_and_Warehouse_Union" title="International Longshore and Warehouse Union">International Longshoremen's and Warehousemen's Union</a> refused to deliver military equipment destined for the Salvadoran government. The leader of the union, Jim Herman, was known as a supporter of Romero and denounced his death.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> On 24 March 1984 a protest was held in <a href="/wiki/Los_Angeles" title="Los Angeles">Los Angeles, California</a> where around 3,000 people, organized by 20 November Coalition, protested US intervention in El Salvador, using the anniversary of the Archbishop's death and his face as a symbol.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> On 24 March 1990, 10,000 people marched in front of the <a href="/wiki/White_House" title="White House">White House</a> to denounce the military aid that was still flowing from the United States to the Salvadoran government. Protestors carried a bust of the archbishop and quoted some of his speeches, in addition to the event being held on the anniversary of his death. Noted figures <a href="/wiki/Ed_Asner" title="Ed Asner">Ed Asner</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jennifer_Casolo" title="Jennifer Casolo">Jennifer Casolo</a> participated in the event.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_response">Government response</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Government response">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h5>
<p>On 25 March 1980, US Secretary of State <a href="/wiki/Cyrus_Vance" title="Cyrus Vance">Cyrus Vance</a> revealed that the White House would continue to fund the Salvadoran government and provide it military aid, in spite of the pleas of Romero and his death immediately prior to this announcement.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> On 31 March 1983, <a href="/wiki/Roberto_D%27Aubuisson" title="Roberto D&#39;Aubuisson">Roberto D'Aubuisson</a> was allowed entry to the United States by the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State" title="United States Department of State">State Department</a> after deeming him not barred from entry any longer. When asked about D'Aubuisson's association with the assassination of Romero, the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State" title="United States Department of State">Department of State</a> responded that "the allegations have not been substantiated."<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> In November 1993, documents by the Department of State, <a href="/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Defense" title="United States Department of Defense">Department of Defense</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Central_Intelligence_Agency" title="Central Intelligence Agency">Central Intelligence Agency</a> were released after pressure by Congress increased. The 12,000 documents revealed that the administrations of <a href="/wiki/Ronald_Reagan" title="Ronald Reagan">Ronald Reagan</a> and <a href="/wiki/George_H._W._Bush" title="George H. W. Bush">George H. W. Bush</a> knew of the assassinations conducted by D'Aubuisson, including that of Romero, yet still worked with him despite this.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Investigations_into_the_assassination">Investigations into the assassination</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Investigations into the assassination">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>To date, no one has ever been prosecuted for the assassination, or confessed to it to police. The gunman had not been identified until 2000.<sup id="cite_ref-elfaro_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-elfaro-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Immediately following the assassination, <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Napole%C3%B3n_Duarte" title="José Napoleón Duarte">José Napoleón Duarte</a>, the newly appointed <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Foreign_Affairs_(El_Salvador)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister of Foreign Affairs (El Salvador)">foreign minister of El Salvador</a>, actively promulgated a "blame on both sides" propaganda trope in order to provide cover for the lack of official inquiry into the assassination plot.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Subsequent investigations by the United Nations and other international bodies have established that the four assassins were members of a death squad led by D'Aubuisson.<sup id="cite_ref-scarend_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-scarend-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> Revelations of the D'Aubuisson plot came to light in 1984 when US ambassador <a href="/wiki/Robert_White_(ambassador)" class="mw-redirect" title="Robert White (ambassador)">Robert White</a> testified before the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Congress" title="United States Congress">United States Congress</a> that "there was sufficient evidence" to convict D'Aubuisson of planning and ordering Romero's assassination.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> In 1993, an official <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> report identified D'Aubuisson as the man who ordered the killing.<sup id="cite_ref-Morozzo_59-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Morozzo-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> D'Aubuisson had strong connections to the <a href="/wiki/Nicaraguan_National_Guard" class="mw-redirect" title="Nicaraguan National Guard">Nicaraguan National Guard</a> and to its offshoot the <a href="/wiki/Fifteenth_of_September_Legion" title="Fifteenth of September Legion">Fifteenth of September Legion</a><sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> and had also planned to overthrow the government in a coup. Later, he founded the political party <a href="/wiki/Nationalist_Republican_Alliance" title="Nationalist Republican Alliance">Nationalist Republican Alliance</a> (ARENA), and organized death squads that systematically carried out politically motivated assassinations and other human rights abuses in El Salvador. Álvaro Rafael Saravia, a former captain in the <a href="/wiki/Salvadoran_Air_Force" title="Salvadoran Air Force">Salvadoran Air Force</a>, was chief of security for D'Aubuisson and an active member of these death squads. In 2003 a United States human rights organization, the <a href="/wiki/Center_for_Justice_and_Accountability" title="Center for Justice and Accountability">Center for Justice and Accountability</a>, filed a civil action against Saravia. In 2004, he was found liable by a <a href="/wiki/US_District_Court" class="mw-redirect" title="US District Court">US District Court</a> under the <a href="/wiki/Alien_Tort_Claims_Act" class="mw-redirect" title="Alien Tort Claims Act">Alien Tort Claims Act</a> (ATCA) (28 U.S.C. §&#160;1350) for aiding, conspiring, and participating in the assassination of Romero. Saravia was ordered to pay $10 million for <a href="/wiki/Extrajudicial_killing" title="Extrajudicial killing">extrajudicial killing</a> and <a href="/wiki/Crimes_against_humanity" title="Crimes against humanity">crimes against humanity</a> pursuant to the ATCA;<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> he has since gone into hiding.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> On 24 March 2010–the thirtieth anniversary of Romero's death–Salvadoran President <a href="/wiki/Mauricio_Funes" title="Mauricio Funes">Mauricio Funes</a> offered an official state apology for Romero's assassination. Speaking before Romero's family, representatives of the Catholic Church, diplomats, and government officials, Funes said those involved in the assassination "unfortunately acted with the protection, collaboration, or participation of state agents."<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A 2000 article by Tom Gibb, then a correspondent with <i>The Guardian</i> and later with the <a href="/wiki/BBC" title="BBC">BBC</a>, attributes the murder to a detective of the Salvadoran National Police named Óscar Pérez Linares, on orders of D'Aubuisson. The article cites an anonymous former death squad member who claimed he had been assigned to guard a house in San Salvador used by a unit of three counter-guerrilla operatives directed by D'Aubuisson. The guard, whom Gibb identified as "Jorge," purported to have witnessed Linares fraternizing with the group, which was nicknamed the "Little Angels," and to have heard them praise Linares for the killing. The article furthermore attributes full knowledge of the assassination to the <a href="/wiki/CIA" class="mw-redirect" title="CIA">CIA</a> as far back as 1983.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-scarend_75-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-scarend-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> The article reports that both Linares and the Little Angels commander, who Jorge identified as "El Negro Mario," were killed by a CIA-trained Salvadoran special police unit in 1986; the unit had been assigned to investigate the murders. In 1983, U.S. Lt. Col. <a href="/wiki/Oliver_North" title="Oliver North">Oliver North</a>, aide to then-Vice President George H.W. Bush, is alleged to have personally requested the Salvadoran military to "remove" Linares and several others from their service. Three years later they were pursued and extrajudicially killed – Linares after being found in neighboring Guatemala. The article cites another source in the Salvadoran military as saying, "they knew far too much to live."<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In a 2010 article for the Salvadoran online newspaper <i><a href="/wiki/El_Faro_(digital_newspaper)" title="El Faro (digital newspaper)">El Faro</a></i>,<sup id="cite_ref-elfaro_73-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-elfaro-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> Saravia was interviewed from a mountain hideout.<sup id="cite_ref-elfaro_73-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-elfaro-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> He named D'Aubuisson as giving the assassination order to him over the phone,<sup id="cite_ref-elfaro_73-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-elfaro-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> and said that he and his cohorts drove the assassin to the chapel and paid him 1,000 <a href="/wiki/Salvadoran_col%C3%B3n" title="Salvadoran colón">Salvadoran colóns</a> after the event.<sup id="cite_ref-elfaro_73-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-elfaro-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In April 2017, however, in the wake of the overruling of a civil war <a href="/wiki/Amnesty" title="Amnesty">amnesty</a> law the previous year, a judge in El Salvador, Rigoberto Chicas, allowed the case against the escaped Saravia's alleged role in the murder of Romero to be reopened. On 23 October 2018, days after Romero's canonization, Judge Chicas issued a new arrest warrant for him, and Interpol and the National Police are charged with finding his hideout and apprehending him.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-thetabletwarrant_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-thetabletwarrant-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> As both D'Aubuisson and Linares had already died, they could not be prosecuted.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="International_recognition">International recognition</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: International recognition">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Tomb_of_%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_y_Gald%C3%A1mez.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Tomb_of_%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_y_Gald%C3%A1mez.jpg/220px-Tomb_of_%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_y_Gald%C3%A1mez.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Tomb_of_%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_y_Gald%C3%A1mez.jpg/330px-Tomb_of_%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_y_Gald%C3%A1mez.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Tomb_of_%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_y_Gald%C3%A1mez.jpg/440px-Tomb_of_%C3%93scar_Arnulfo_Romero_y_Gald%C3%A1mez.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3024" data-file-height="4032" /></a><figcaption>Romero's tomb as seen in 2021.</figcaption></figure>
<p>During his first visit to El Salvador in 1983, <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> entered the cathedral in San Salvador and prayed at Romero's tomb, despite opposition from the government and from some within the church who strongly opposed liberation theology. Afterwards, the Pope praised Romero as a "zealous and venerated pastor who tried to stop violence." John Paul II also asked for dialogue between the government and opposition to end El Salvador's civil war.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 7 May 2000, in Rome's Colosseum during the <a href="/wiki/Great_Jubilee" title="Great Jubilee">Jubilee Year</a> celebrations, Pope John Paul II commemorated 20th century martyrs. Of the several categories of martyrs, the seventh consisted of Christians who were killed for defending their brethren in the Americas. Despite the opposition of some social conservatives within the church, John Paul II insisted that Romero be included. He asked the organizers of the event to proclaim Romero "that great witness of the Gospel."<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 21 December 2010, the United Nations General Assembly proclaimed 24 March as the International Day for the Right to the Truth concerning Gross Human Rights Violations and for the Dignity of Victims which recognizes, in particular, the important work and values of Romero.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 22 March 2011, U.S. President <a href="/wiki/Barack_Obama" title="Barack Obama">Barack Obama</a> visited Romero's tomb during an official visit to El Salvador.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> Irish President <a href="/wiki/Michael_D._Higgins" title="Michael D. Higgins">Michael D. Higgins</a> visited the cathedral and tomb of Romero on 25 October 2013 during a state visit to El Salvador.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Famed linguist <a href="/wiki/Noam_Chomsky" title="Noam Chomsky">Noam Chomsky</a> speaks highly of Romero's social work, and refers often to his murder.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> In 2014, El Salvador's main international airport was named after him, becoming <a href="/wiki/El_Salvador_International_Airport" title="El Salvador International Airport">Monseñor Óscar Arnulfo Romero y Galdámez International Airport</a>, and later, San Óscar Arnulfo Romero y Galdámez International Airport in 2018 after his canonization.<sup id="cite_ref-name_change_2018_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-name_change_2018-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Romero is <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">remembered</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> and in the <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Episcopal_Church)" title="Calendar of saints (Episcopal Church)">Episcopal Church</a> on 24 March.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Sainthood">Sainthood</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Sainthood">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Process_for_beatification">Process for beatification</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Process for beatification">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_(17393112443).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_%2817393112443%29.jpg/220px-BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_%2817393112443%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_%2817393112443%29.jpg/330px-BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_%2817393112443%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_%2817393112443%29.jpg/440px-BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_%2817393112443%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1500" data-file-height="1000" /></a><figcaption>Savior of the World Plaza at the beatification</figcaption></figure>
<p>Romero's sainthood cause at the Vatican was opened in 1993, but the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_News_Service" title="Catholic News Service">Catholic News Service</a> reported that it "was delayed for years as the Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith studied his writings, amid wider debate over whether he had been killed for his faith or for political reasons."<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In March 2005, <a href="/wiki/Vincenzo_Paglia" title="Vincenzo Paglia">Vincenzo Paglia</a>, the Vatican official in charge of the process, announced that Romero's cause had cleared a theological audit by the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith">Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith</a>, at the time headed by Cardinal Joseph Ratzinger (later elected <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a>) and that beatification could follow within six months.<sup id="cite_ref-cwnews_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cwnews-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> died within weeks of those remarks. Predictably, the transition of the new pontiff slowed down the work of canonizations and beatifications. Pope Benedict instituted changes that had the overall effect of reining in the Vatican's so-called "factory of saints."<sup id="cite_ref-timeproxy_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-timeproxy-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> In an October 2005 interview, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Saraiva_Martins" title="José Saraiva Martins">José Saraiva Martins</a>, the Prefect of the Congregation for the Causes of Saints, was asked if Paglia's predictions of a clearance for Romero's beatification remained on track. Saraiva responded, "Not as far as I know today,"<sup id="cite_ref-saraiva_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-saraiva-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> In November 2005, the Jesuit magazine <i><a href="/wiki/La_Civilt%C3%A0_Cattolica" title="La Civiltà Cattolica">La Civiltà Cattolica</a></i> signaled that Romero's beatification was still "years away."<sup id="cite_ref-cn-0506300_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cn-0506300-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Although Benedict XVI had always been a fierce critic of liberation theology, Paglia reported in December 2012 that the Pope had informed him of the decision to "unblock" the cause and allow it to move forward.<sup id="cite_ref-catholicnews.com_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholicnews.com-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> However, no progress was made before Benedict's resignation in February 2013. <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a> was elected in March 2013, and in September 2013, Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Gerhard_Ludwig_M%C3%BCller" title="Gerhard Ludwig Müller">Gerhard Ludwig Müller</a>, <a href="/wiki/Prefect" title="Prefect">Prefect</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Doctrine_of_the_Faith" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith">Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith</a>, stated that the Vatican doctrinal office has been "given the greenlight" to pursue sainthood for Romero.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Beatification">Beatification</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Beatification">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_(18010267052).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_%2818010267052%29.jpg/220px-BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_%2818010267052%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_%2818010267052%29.jpg/330px-BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_%2818010267052%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_%2818010267052%29.jpg/440px-BEATIFICACI%C3%93N_DE_MONSE%C3%91OR_OSCAR_ARNULFO_ROMERO_%2818010267052%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="1333" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatification</a> celebration on 23 May 2015 in San Salvador</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 18 August 2014, Pope Francis said that "[t]he process [of beatification of Romero] was at the Congregation for the Doctrine of the Faith, blocked for 'prudential reasons', so they said.  Now it is unblocked." Francis stated that "There are no doctrinal problems and it is very important that [the beatification] is done quickly."<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> The beatification signaled Francis' affirmation of Romero's work with the poor and as a major change in the direction of the church since he was elected.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In January 2015, an advisory panel to the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a> voted unanimously to recognize Romero as a martyr, and the cardinals who were voting members of the Congregation unanimously recommended to Francis that he be beatified as a martyr (a martyr can be beatified without recognition of a miracle).<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> Archbishop <a href="/wiki/Vincenzo_Paglia" title="Vincenzo Paglia">Vincenzo Paglia</a>, the postulator (chief promoter) of the causes of saints, said that Romero's assassination at the altar was intended "to strike the Church that flowed from the Second Vatican Council" and that the motive for his murder "was not caused by motives that were simply political, but by hatred for a faith that, imbued with charity, would not be silent in the face of the injustices that relentlessly and cruelly slaughtered the poor and their defenders."<sup id="cite_ref-catholicnews.com_102-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-catholicnews.com-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> On 3 February 2015, Francis received Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Angelo_Amato" title="Angelo Amato">Angelo Amato</a>, <a href="/wiki/Prefect#Catholic_Church" title="Prefect">Prefect</a> of the Congregation for the Causes of Saints, in a private audience, and authorized Amato to promulgate (officially authorize) Romero's decree of martyrdom, meaning it had gained the Congregation's voting members and the Pope's approval. This cleared the way for the Pope to later set a date for his beatification.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The beatification of Romero was held in San Salvador on 23 May 2015 in the Plaza Salvador del Mundo under the <a href="/wiki/Monumento_al_Divino_Salvador_del_Mundo" title="Monumento al Divino Salvador del Mundo">Monumento al Divino Salvador del Mundo</a>. Amato presided over the ceremony on behalf of Francis, who in a letter to Archbishop of San Salvador <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Luis_Escobar_Alas" title="José Luis Escobar Alas">José Luis Escobar Alas</a> marked the occasion by calling Romero "a voice that continues to resonate."<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> An estimated 250,000 people attended the service,<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> many watching on large television screens set up in the streets around the plaza.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonization">Canonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=%C3%93scar_Romero&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Canonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._(44398703435).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._%2844398703435%29.jpg/220px-Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._%2844398703435%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._%2844398703435%29.jpg/330px-Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._%2844398703435%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._%2844398703435%29.jpg/440px-Ceremonia_de_Canonizaci%C3%B3n_de_Monse%C3%B1or_Romero._%2844398703435%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6720" data-file-height="4480" /></a><figcaption>Canonization Mass celebrated on 14 October 2018 in Saint Peter's Square.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Three miracles were submitted to the Congregation for the Causes of Saints in Rome in October 2016 that could have led to Romero's canonization. But each of these miracles was rejected after being investigated. A fourth (concerning the pregnant woman Cecilia Maribel Flores) was investigated in a diocesan process in San Salvador that was opened on 31 January 2017 and which concluded its initial investigation on 28 February before documentation was submitted to Rome via the apostolic nunciature. The CCS validated this on 7 April.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> On 11 August, Paglia celebrated the Romero Centenary Mass in <a href="/wiki/St_George%27s_Cathedral,_Southwark" title="St George&#39;s Cathedral, Southwark">St George's Cathedral, Southwark</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> in London, where the cross and relics of Romero are preserved.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Subsequently, medical experts issued unanimous approval to the presented miracle on 26 October with theologians also confirming their approval on 14 December. The CCS members likewise approved the case on 6 February 2018. Pope Francis approved this miracle on 6 March 2018, allowing for Romero to be canonized and the date was announced at a consistory of cardinals held on 19 May.
